- Robertson's harmony places Jesus calls fishermen to follow him (section 41) before Herod throws John the Baptist into prison (section 71), against the fact "Jesus went to Galilee after John's arrest, and there called the fishermen." **Resolved:** Robertson's section 71 is Mark 6's flashback telling of John's arrest, placed where the Gospels tell it, not where it happened; Mark 1:14 and Matthew 4:12 put the arrest before Jesus' Galilean preaching. `harmony-order:john-imprisoned>call-fishermen`
- Robertson's harmony places Jesus preaches the Sermon on the Mount (section 54) before Herod throws John the Baptist into prison (section 71), against the fact "When John was imprisoned Jesus withdrew to Galilee and began to preach; the Sermon on the Mount came after." **Resolved:** Robertson's section 71 is Mark 6's flashback telling of John's arrest, placed where the Gospels tell it, not where it happened; Mark 1:14 and Matthew 4:12 put the arrest before Jesus' Galilean preaching. `harmony-order:john-imprisoned>sermon-mount`
- Robertson's harmony places Jesus calms a storm on the lake (section 65) before Herod throws John the Baptist into prison (section 71), against the fact "Jesus' Galilean ministry, which included calming the storm, began after John's arrest." **Resolved:** Robertson's section 71 is Mark 6's flashback telling of John's arrest, placed where the Gospels tell it, not where it happened; Mark 1:14 and Matthew 4:12 put the arrest before Jesus' Galilean preaching. `harmony-order:john-imprisoned>calms-storm`

## Order facts

| Earlier | Later | Why | Verse | Close |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| God creates the heavens and the earth | God forms Adam from the dust of the ground | Genesis opens with God creating the heavens and the earth, and He made man on the sixth day. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 1:27; Genesis 1:31 | yes |
| God creates the heavens and the earth | God makes Eve from Adam's rib | God created the heavens and the earth 'in the beginning'; Eve was made from a rib taken from Adam. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 2:21–22 | yes |
| God creates the heavens and the earth | Adam and Eve are sent out of Eden | God created the heavens and the earth 'in the beginning'; Adam and Eve were sent out of Eden before they had any children. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 3:23; Genesis 4:1 | yes |
| God creates the heavens and the earth | Cain kills his brother Abel | God created the heavens and the earth 'in the beginning'; Abel, Adam and Eve's second son, was killed by Cain before Seth was born. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 4:8; Genesis 4:25 |  |
| God creates the heavens and the earth | Seth is born to Adam and Eve | God created the heavens and the earth 'in the beginning'; Seth was born when Adam was 130. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 5:3 |  |
| God creates the heavens and the earth | Enoch walks with God and is taken away | God created the heavens and the earth 'in the beginning'; Enoch, seventh from Adam and great-grandfather of Noah, was taken at 365. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 5:23–24; Jude 1:14 |  |
| God creates the heavens and the earth | Lamech's son Noah is born | God created the heavens and the earth 'in the beginning'; Noah was born in the tenth generation from Adam. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 5:28–29 |  |
| God creates the heavens and the earth | God tells Noah to build an ark | God created the heavens and the earth 'in the beginning'; the ark was commanded once Noah, past 500, had grown sons with wives. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 5:32; Genesis 6:18 |  |
| God creates the heavens and the earth | The great Flood begins | God created the heavens and the earth 'in the beginning'; the Flood came in Noah's 600th year. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 7:6 |  |
| God creates the heavens and the earth | Noah's ark comes to rest on Ararat | God created the heavens and the earth 'in the beginning'; the ark came to rest on Ararat in the seventh month of the Flood. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 8:4 |  |
| God creates the heavens and the earth | God sets the rainbow as His covenant sign | God created the heavens and the earth 'in the beginning'; the rainbow covenant was made when Noah's family came out of the ark. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 8:18; Genesis 9:12–13 |  |
| God creates the heavens and the earth | Noah curses Canaan after his drunkenness | God created the heavens and the earth 'in the beginning'; Noah cursed Canaan after the Flood, once he had planted a vineyard. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 9:20; Genesis 9:25 |  |
| God creates the heavens and the earth | God confuses the languages at Babel | God created the heavens and the earth 'in the beginning'; Babel was built by Noah's descendants as they spread out after the Flood. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 10:32; Genesis 11:9 |  |
| God creates the heavens and the earth | Terah moves his family from Ur to Haran | God created the heavens and the earth 'in the beginning'; Terah, born generations after the Flood in Shem's line, moved his family to Haran. | Genesis 1:1; Genesis 11:10; Genesis 11:31 |  |
| God forms Adam from the dust of the ground | God makes Eve from Adam's rib | Adam was made on the sixth day of creation; Eve was made from a rib taken from Adam. | Genesis 1:31; Genesis 2:7; Genesis 2:21–22 | yes |
| God forms Adam from the dust of the ground | Adam and Eve are sent out of Eden | Adam was made on the sixth day of creation; Adam and Eve were sent out of Eden before they had any children. | Genesis 1:31; Genesis 2:7; Genesis 3:23; Genesis 4:1 | yes |
| God forms Adam from the dust of the ground | Cain kills his brother Abel | Adam was made on the sixth day of creation; Abel, Adam and Eve's second son, was killed by Cain before Seth was born. | Genesis 1:31; Genesis 2:7; Genesis 4:8; Genesis 4:25 |  |
| God forms Adam from the dust of the ground | Seth is born to Adam and Eve | Adam was made on the sixth day of creation; Seth was born when Adam was 130. | Genesis 1:31; Genesis 2:7; Genesis 5:3 |  |
| God forms Adam from the dust of the ground | Enoch walks with God and is taken away | Adam was made on the sixth day of creation; Enoch, seventh from Adam and great-grandfather of Noah, was taken at 365. | Genesis 1:31; Genesis 2:7; Genesis 5:23–24; Jude 1:14 |  |

## Never dealt together

| These | Against these | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Enoch walks with God and is taken away | Lamech's son Noah is born | The Hebrew text of Genesis 5 has Enoch taken 69 years before Noah was born; the Samaritan text's smaller ages at fatherhood put Noah's birth about 180 years before Enoch was taken. |
| Noah curses Canaan after his drunkenness | God confuses the languages at Babel; Terah moves his family from Ur to Haran | Genesis does not say how long after the Flood Noah planted his vineyard (he lived 350 years after it, Genesis 9:28), so its time against Babel and Terah's move is not stated. |
| Ezra arrives in Jerusalem | Nehemiah asks the king to send him to Judah; Nehemiah rebuilds Jerusalem's walls; Nehemiah throws Tobiah out of the temple | Ezra 7:7's 'seventh year of Artaxerxes' is 458 BC (Artaxerxes I, the traditional view) or 398 BC (Artaxerxes II, held by many scholars since van Hoonacker); Nehemiah came in Artaxerxes I's twentieth year, 445/444 BC, and returned after his thirty-second, 433/432 BC. |
| Israel goes to war against Benjamin | Othniel rescues Israel from King Cushan; Left-handed Ehud kills King Eglon; Deborah and Barak defeat Sisera; Gideon tests God with a fleece; Gideon's 300 men rout the Midianites; A woman's millstone kills Abimelech; Jephthah vows, then defeats Ammon; Samson tears a lion apart; Delilah has Samson's head shaved; Samson pulls down Dagon's temple | Judges 17–21 is set early (Phinehas, Aaron's grandson, is priest, Judges 20:28) but is not tied to any judge, so its order against each judge's story is unknown. Othniel is included because nothing orders the war against him either. |
| Ruth comes to Bethlehem with Naomi; Boaz marries Ruth | Othniel rescues Israel from King Cushan; Left-handed Ehud kills King Eglon; Deborah and Barak defeat Sisera; Gideon tests God with a fleece; Gideon's 300 men rout the Midianites; A woman's millstone kills Abimelech; Jephthah vows, then defeats Ammon; Samson tears a lion apart; Delilah has Samson's head shaved; Samson pulls down Dagon's temple; Hannah gives birth to Samuel; God calls the boy Samuel at night; The Philistines capture the ark | Ruth's story is set 'in the days when the judges ruled' (Ruth 1:1), but under which judge is not said; Eli and Samuel were judges too (1 Samuel 4:18; 7:15). |
| Daniel is thrown into the lions' den | Cyrus lets the exiles go home | Both fall in the first years after Babylon fell; Daniel 6:28 names the reigns of Darius and Cyrus without ordering these events, and who 'Darius the Mede' was is itself debated. |
| Simeon holds the baby Jesus in the temple | Wise men bring gifts to the child Jesus; Joseph flees to Egypt with Mary and Jesus; Herod kills the boys of Bethlehem | Luke has the family go home to Nazareth after the presentation (Luke 2:39); Matthew has the wise men's visit and the flight to Egypt. How the two accounts fit together is debated. |
| Jesus preaches the Sermon on the Mount | Jesus feeds the five thousand | The Gospels arrange Galilean events differently, and neither event is tied in time to the other. |
